Version 1.0 | 2026-11-01 | Planning document - not a finished course or an official JLPT syllabus

DOCUMENT STATUS
Owner: FreeJapaneseAcademy. Audience: English-speaking learners with no prior Japanese through advanced study.
Purpose: A build-ready editorial and instructional specification for complete courses, reusable study centers,
original exercises, real-language practice and transparent local progress tracking.
Approval status: Draft for pedagogical review. Counts are editorial planning targets, not published JLPT requirements.

==========================================================================================================
01 | MISSION, ACCURACY BOUNDARIES AND GRADUATE OUTCOMES
==========================================================================================================
MISSION
Build a permanently free, self-paced Japanese-language academy in which students learn to understand,
read, listen to, write, and optionally speak Japanese. Cover preparation for the five JLPT levels while
prioritizing practical communicative competence. A separate Level 0 is compulsory to offer but not a
hard prerequisite for experienced learners.

NON-NEGOTIABLE ACADEMIC DISTINCTIONS
* The JLPT tests language knowledge, reading and listening. It does not directly assess speaking or writing.
  FreeJapaneseAcademy will teach writing and optional speaking as additional practical competencies.
* Organizers do NOT publish a current exhaustive official grammar, kanji or vocabulary list by JLPT level.
  Our lists, level tags and volume targets are academy-authored, reviewed approximations; label them as such.
* Never present a raw percentage from our original practice tests as an official JLPT scaled score or as
  a guaranteed predictor of passing an actual JLPT exam.
* A JLPT-aligned course is not an accredited qualification. Use the phrase 'original JLPT-style practice'
  for academy-created questions, not 'official JLPT exam'.
* Official Can-do self-evaluation lists are descriptive survey references, not a prescribed syllabus.
* Do not claim a fixed equivalence between JLPT and CEFR speaking/writing abilities.

PEDAGOGICAL FOUNDATIONS
1. Can-do first: each chapter describes an observable communicative task, then assigns language input.
2. Spiral progression: vocabulary, kanji, grammar and skills recur in increasingly difficult contexts.
3. Input plus output: reading and listening in every chapter, writing in every chapter, optional speaking.
4. Controlled -> guided -> independent practice with immediate, accurate feedback.
5. Transparent progress: completion, quiz performance, delayed recall and practical Can-do assessments differ.
6. Minimum cognitive overload: concise study steps; optional deep-dive reference pages are freely accessible.
7. Context and nuance: 'meaning' alone is not mastery; register, usage, collocation and cultural context matter.
8. Learner autonomy: no forced paywall, and ability to skip or revisit lessons without breaking progress.

LEVEL EXIT OUTCOMES (ACADEMY-AUTHORED TARGETS)
L0  Can identify and reproduce basic kana, handle long vowels and contractions, type kana, understand
    simple greetings and decode very short supported phrases without relying on romaji.
N5  Can understand and produce simple daily-life messages and conversations; recognize frequent starter
    kanji in context; read notices, short dialogue and schedules; write a short self-introduction.
N4  Can follow connected everyday texts/conversations and describe routines, changes, experiences,
    reasons and plans, using appropriate elementary politeness and common kanji words.
N3  Can follow more natural connected talk and longer texts, infer straightforward implied meaning,
    give reasons and structured short opinions, and handle familiar work and service situations.
N2  Can read complex written Japanese and follow coherent natural-speed discussions in varied situations;
    evaluate arguments and handle formal practical language; write balanced summaries and responses.
N1  Can analyze dense abstract texts, complex lectures, advanced public discourse and rhetorical nuance;
    distinguish subtle lexicon/register and synthesize multi-source material in extended written responses.

SPEAKING AND WRITING MILESTONES
At L0/N5, prioritize kana writing/typing and short guided utterances. Through N4/N3 introduce coherent
paragraphs and interaction; through N2/N1 introduce nuanced, source-grounded writing and discussion.
The academy must avoid equating a completed speaking self-check with independent verified proficiency.

==========================================================================================================
02 | SCOPE, INVENTORY BUDGET AND COUNTING RULES
==========================================================================================================
All numbers below are PROVISIONAL academy production targets, not official JLPT item counts.
One chapter has EIGHT micro-lesson slots (including an optional speaking slot) PLUS a chapter checkpoint.
A "new word" is a unique normalized lexical entry first introduced at this level; later appearances are reuse.
A "new kanji" is a unique character first introduced for reading recognition at the level, not each reading.
A "grammar target" is a distinct usage/contrast introduced for teaching, not necessarily a discrete JLPT item.
One "core listening asset" is a recorded scenario/dialogue/monologue, NOT individual pronunciation snippets.
One "core reading" is a unique authored passage, message, article or task with comprehension checks.

Level   Modules Chapters   Slots   Words+  Grammar+   Kanji+  Readings   Audio  Mocks
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
L0            5       20     160      130        12        0        20      40      0
N5           10       40     320      850        95      110        60      80      2
N4           10       40     320     1050       130      230        60      80      2
N3           12       48     384     1650       170      360        72      96      3
N2           12       48     384     2200       190      490        96      96      3
N1           13       52     416     2700       230      810       104     104      4
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TOTAL: 62 modules | 248 chapters | 1984 micro-lesson slots (+ 248 chapter checkpoints)
Target new lexical entries: 8,580; grammar targets: 827; new kanji: 2,000.
Core reading assets: 412; core listening assets: 496.
Assessments: 248 chapter checks + 62 module checkpoints + 32 cumulative milestone reviews + 14 original full-length mocks.
Also plan at least one writing task and one optional speaking task per chapter, with model answers and rubrics.
Spaced-repetition review cards are generated from reviewed knowledge objects; do not count them as new words.
Every core audio track requires transcript, speaker/voice metadata, pronunciation QA and accessible controls.
CAPACITY GUARDRAILS
Vocabulary items can be introduced both in core chapters and linked study-center sets. The center is
not a second dictionary with duplicated IDs. At advanced levels, supplement core chapter lessons with
structured topical word packs to achieve the above vocabulary inventory without overloading any single
lesson. For N1, 2,700 new entries distributed across 52 chapters would exceed 50 new entries/chapter if
all introduced in one go: divide into small staged batches, continue revisiting earlier items, and allow
learner-selected supplementary decks. New kanji figures are recognition targets; compulsory handwriting
of every character is NOT required. These numbers are planning ceilings/targets subject to expert review.

BASIC PER-CHAPTER CONTENT PACK (SCOPE, NOT A FIXED DAILY STUDY QUOTA)
- Lesson 01: context, goals, first exposure and a Can-do preview.
- Lesson 02: vocabulary and word study in useful examples.
- Lesson 03: grammar and sentence-pattern lesson with contrasts and exercises.
- Lesson 04: script/kanji and orthographic application; kana-only at Level 0.
- Lesson 05: a graded reading asset with questions, glossary and optional reading aids.
- Lesson 06: listening with transcript and focused comprehension tasks; two distinct core audio tracks.
- Lesson 07: supported writing/typing production with an explicit assessment rubric.
- Lesson 08: optional speaking or shadowing, model audio and honest self-assessment rubric.
- CHK: 10-25 original questions and a feedback summary, with at least vocabulary, grammar/script,
  reading and listening represented wherever developmentally appropriate.
- Supplement: link to spaced-repetition review queue, relevant reference entries and any optional texts.
At L0 adjust lessons 02-04 toward script, auditory discrimination and tracing; no invented kanji tasks.

==========================================================================================================
03 | TEACHING SEQUENCE AND ASSESSABLE LESSON DESIGN
==========================================================================================================
STANDARD CHAPTER RECIPE
Start with one concrete situation and visible Can-do claim. Introduce only the language necessary
for initial success; retain optional deep-dive tabs for the full linguistic explanation. Then provide:
(a) input and noticing, (b) form and controlled practice, (c) word/script reinforcement,
(d) reading, (e) listening, (f) productive writing, (g) optional speaking, (h) mixed checkpoint.
Every lesson must specify prerequisite IDs, objectives, new/review items, asset IDs, question IDs,
expected duration range, difficulty, script settings, accessibility metadata and content provenance.

REVIEW SCHEDULE
Use an open-source spaced repetition algorithm (e.g. FSRS) to schedule recognition cards and,
where appropriate, productive recall cards. Avoid promising deterministic review intervals. New words
and kanji are tagged by source chapter, but their scheduling is personalized across all study centers.
Give learners choices: review now, snooze, mark as known, reintroduce, or reset a card with confirmation.
Recognize kana/kanji visual confusions and expose targeted contrast practice.

EXERCISE FORMAT CATALOG
Level 0: kana-to-sound, sound-to-kana, similar-character identification, IME typing, short dictation.
N5/N4: form choice, particles, word order, vocabulary in context, mini-dialogue, information finding,
       true/false with evidence, dictation fragments, sentence construction.
N3: grammar contrasts, clause linking, short textual inference, speaker purpose, paraphrase, topic tracking.
N2/N1: register and nuance choice, multi-source synthesis, argument mapping, distractor diagnosis,
       detailed contextual usage, natural-speed listening and long text inference.
Cross-level: word-family drill, listening at adjustable speeds, writing with model/rubric, optional speaking.

LEVEL-APPROPRIATE SUPPORT
L0/N5: always offer furigana and English; allow temporary romaji as a scaffold for L0 only.
N4: furigana on demand for new kanji; English explanations for difficulties.
N3: Japanese paraphrases with English explanations available; more authentic input.
N2/N1: Japanese-first examples and definitions where feasible; precise English notes on nuance.
Never use furigana that changes the intended reading of a word or unnecessarily reveals answer choices.
Use ruby markup for accessible furigana; provide text alternatives and keyboard-operable hide/show controls.

AUDIO POLICY
Speak naturally and intelligibly at the appropriate level; beginner recordings may be slower but
avoid unnatural phonology. Produce high-quality licensed original or properly permitted speech; include
transcript and a human-review workflow. Do not use unlicensed drama, news or music recordings.

WRITING POLICY
Support IME typing on mobile and desktop, optional handwriting tracing for kana/kanji, and open-text
responses from N5 onward. Exact-match auto-grading is unsuitable for free writing. Offer examples,
criteria and constructive self-review; add human evaluation only if a future moderation process exists.

SPEAKING POLICY
Optional shadowing, playback and locally stored recordings (only if explicitly initiated). Browser
speech recognition, if added, must be labelled as unreliable and not a certified pronunciation grade.
Provide model audio, timing cues, fluency/clarity checklist and alternatives when microphones unavailable.

==========================================================================================================
04 | NINE DEDICATED STUDY CENTERS: FUNCTIONAL SPECIFICATION
==========================================================================================================
01. Hiragana
  FUNCTIONS: 46 core symbols, voiced variants, contracted sounds, long vowels and small tsu; handwriting,
  IME typing, reading fluency and confusing-symbol sets
  METRICS:   Recognition accuracy, sound contrast accuracy, typing fluency and review due
  CROSS-LINK: use existing item IDs; link to its first introduction, later appearances and review queue.

02. Katakana
  FUNCTIONS: 46 core symbols, variants, long vowel mark, loanword conventions, extended borrowed sounds,
  confusable forms and realistic signs/menus
  METRICS:   Recognition, transcription and common loanword-reading success
  CROSS-LINK: use existing item IDs; link to its first introduction, later appearances and review queue.

03. Kanji
  FUNCTIONS: Character pages with meaning, readings in actual words, components, stroke diagrams if
  licensed, compounds, homographs, confusables and progression tags
  METRICS:   Recognition of kanji words; context-aware reading; optional writing
  CROSS-LINK: use existing item IDs; link to its first introduction, later appearances and review queue.

04. Vocabulary and Word Study
  FUNCTIONS: Single lexical database with inflections, meanings by sense, pitch/accent evidence when
  reviewed, collocations, register, near-synonyms, antonyms, sentence examples and deep dives
  METRICS:   Distinct word-sense recognition and word-use recall; personalized FSRS
  CROSS-LINK: use existing item IDs; link to its first introduction, later appearances and review queue.

05. Grammar
  FUNCTIONS: Pattern index with function, structure, formation, restrictions, similar forms,
  positive/negative examples, contrast tables, linked chapter IDs
  METRICS:   Form selection in context, sentence composition and error diagnosis
  CROSS-LINK: use existing item IDs; link to its first introduction, later appearances and review queue.

06. Reading
  FUNCTIONS: Level-filtered texts, comprehension, unknown word lookup, optional furigana,
  highlight/notes, genres and progressive length
  METRICS:   Reading accuracy, inference and completion history; no page-view-as-mastery
  CROSS-LINK: use existing item IDs; link to its first introduction, later appearances and review queue.

07. Listening
  FUNCTIONS: Original captioned audio, transcripts, replay/loop, optional controlled speed, dictation,
  note-taking and item-based tasks
  METRICS:   Listening comprehension, repeat errors and interpretation after transcript reveal
  CROSS-LINK: use existing item IDs; link to its first introduction, later appearances and review queue.

08. Writing
  FUNCTIONS: Kana practice, sentence builder, guided composition, paragraphs, essay briefs and
  self-evaluation against rubrics
  METRICS:   Draft/complete state, checklist criteria; no fabricated machine score for prose
  CROSS-LINK: use existing item IDs; link to its first introduction, later appearances and review queue.

09. Speaking (optional)
  FUNCTIONS: Pronunciation comparisons, shadowing, role-play scripts, optional local recording/playback,
  model responses and self-assessment
  METRICS:   Optional practice sessions and self-report; no unsupported proficiency claims
  CROSS-LINK: use existing item IDs; link to its first introduction, later appearances and review queue.

WORD STUDY / VOCABULARY POLICY
A single word entry has one primary identifier per lemma plus normalized reading and sense disambiguation.
A surface form, inflection or contextual sense should not spawn a duplicate study-card count. Linking the
same word from 3 chapters must create ONE saved vocabulary object and appropriately distinct learning
activities. Add a 'deep dive' view for transitivity, conjugation, register, collocation, near-synonyms,
and usage caution. Dictionary data is supplementary; original English teaching explanations are required.

KANJI POLICY
Distinguish: (a) recognition of the written character, (b) reading it in real words, (c) meaning of
related lexical items, (d) optional character handwriting. For each new kanji, introduce at least two
useful words when natural, rather than requiring an exhaustive on/kun memorization list. Review compounds
and visually confusable characters. Kanji are suggested academy-level targets, not official JLPT lists.

==========================================================================================================
05 | EXAMS, MASTERY, LOCAL STORAGE AND ANALYTICS
==========================================================================================================
ASSESSMENT HIERARCHY
1. Immediate lesson checks: 3-8 formative, low-stakes items with explanations and retries.
2. Chapter checkpoint: usually 10-25 original multi-skill items. Count and weight by objectives,
   not by whatever is easiest to auto-grade.
3. Module checkpoint: cumulative application across four chapters, mixed reading/listening and
   short writing task; optional speaking independent from JLPT score.
4. Cumulative milestone review: revisit older material and previously missed misconceptions.
5. Level capstone: real-world integrated task with written portfolio evidence and optional oral output.
6. Original JLPT-style practice examinations: timed and untimed practice modes, accurate level-specific
   test-item families and durations checked against current official documentation before publishing.
   Maintain answer keys, evidence, detailed rationales, distractor validation and no copyrighted reuse.

PROPOSED INTERNAL READINESS RULES (ACADEMY-SPECIFIC; NOT JLPT PASS MARKS)
- Chapter 'completed': learner reaches final page and submits required practice.
- Chapter 'demonstrated': >=80% on an unseen mixed checkpoint, optionally with minimum reading/listening
  coverage. Threshold may change with calibration; do not force a hard lock on the next chapter.
- Module 'ready': >=80% on mixed review, plus completion of a practical task and a spaced review.
- Level 'course complete': all required chapter units finished and capstone attempted.
- Level 'review recommended': weak subskills flagged by performance across time, not a one-time score.
- Original mock reports: number correct, percent correct and per-section practice diagnostic; do NOT
  call scores 'JLPT official scaled scores', 'JLPT certification' or 'guaranteed pass probability'.
- Free-writing self-check is not machine-certified; speaking is optional and never a prerequisite.

PROGRESS RECORDS (BROWSER-LOCAL FIRST)
Storage engine: IndexedDB with versioned schema and Dexie. Use IDs, content version, timestamps and
activity types rather than progress tied to mutable URL strings. Keep per-item study state separately
from aggregate course completion. Save attempts, answers, time spent if user consents, confidence ratings,
flashcard scheduler state, bookmarked items, drafts (opt-in for longer writing), and last-visited lesson.
Offer JSON export, validated import with versioned migrations and a clear-all confirmation. Warn users
that clearing browser storage/private mode or moving devices can lose or separate records. No account
or cross-device synchronization in the initial architecture; never claim otherwise.

PRIVACY BY DESIGN
Do not upload microphone recordings or learner-produced writing to a server by default. Avoid storing
personal data in analytics; provide plain-language disclosure for local storage and any future external
media or analytics. Avoid exposing minors' learning data through public URLs or tracking identifiers.

==========================================================================================================
06 | CONTENT MODEL, INTERNAL LINKING, SEO/GEO AND ACCESSIBILITY
==========================================================================================================
CANONICAL CONTENT TYPES AND RECOMMENDED FIELDS
lesson: id, level, moduleId, chapterId, order, title, slug, version, objectiveIds, prerequisites,
        publishedStatus, estimatedMinutes, blocks, vocabularyIds, kanjiIds, grammarIds, assetIds,
        exerciseIds, optional, author, nativeReviewStatus, updatedAt, citations/licensing.
chapter: id, level, moduleId, title, canDo, objectives, lessonIds, checkpointId, reviewDeckIds,
         readingIds, listeningIds, writingPromptId, optionalSpeakingId, publicationStatus.
lexeme: id, headword, reading, partOfSpeech, senses[], register, levelFirstIntroduced, examples[],
        conjugationClass, collocations[], audioIds[], source/rights metadata.
kanji: id, character, meanings[], components[], readings[], exampleLexemeIds[], strokeAsset,
       confusableIds[], firstTeachingLevel, source/rights metadata.
grammar: id, title, communicativeFunction, formation, constraints[], examples[], contrasts[],
         commonErrors[], firstTeachingLevel, lessonIds[], reviewer.
reading: id, genre, Japanese text, segmentation, questionIds[], glossaryIds[], difficulty, rights.
listening: id, audioAsset, transcript, scene, speed, speakerMetadata, questionIds[], rights, QA.
exercise: id, prompt, format, skills[], knowledgeItemIds[], validResponses, rationale,
          distractorRationales[], difficulty, rubrics, version, publicationStatus.
assessment: id, mode, blueprint, questionIds, sectionRules, answerKeyVersion, timing,
            scoringMethod, reviewRules, qualityReport.

IDENTIFIER CONVENTION
- Stable IDs: l0-m01-c01-l01, n5-m01-c01-l03, gram-n5-topic-particle, vocab-taberu-verb-1,
  kanji-98df (Unicode code point hex), read-n5-m01-c01-a, audio-n5-m01-c01-b.
- Slug can change with an HTTP redirect, but an identifier must never be silently reallocated.
- Maintain canonical entity IDs across courses and study centers.
- Separate published URLs from editor-only drafts to avoid low-value indexable pages.

RECOMMENDED PUBLIC INFORMATION ARCHITECTURE
/learn/                             Guided overview
/learn/start/                       Foundations
/learn/n5/ through /learn/n1/      Level overviews
/learn/n5/module-01/chapter-01/     Chapter overview
/learn/n5/module-01/chapter-01/lesson-01/  Lesson content
/study/hiragana/ and /study/katakana/     Script learning centers
/study/kanji/ and /kanji/<character>/    Kanji reference
/study/vocabulary/ and /words/<slug>/   Word study
/grammar/<slug>/                 Reusable grammar reference
/practice/reading/, /practice/listening/, /practice/writing/, /practice/speaking/
/exams/ and /exams/n5/          Original practice exam overviews
/dashboard/                      Personal browser-local learning dashboard

SEARCH AND AI-DISCOVERABILITY POLICIES
Index only complete, useful, editor-approved original learning pages. Provide concise answers in the
opening paragraph, structured examples with translations and readings, clear limitations and links to
prerequisite/reference material. Include unique title, meta description, canonical URL, breadcrumbs,
accurate learning-level and content-version metadata. Avoid duplicating identical explanations in each
course and reference page. Schema.org annotations must describe real visible content, not invented
reviews, course certifications, ratings or organizations. Sitemap only approved canonical URLs.
No promises of appearing in generative AI responses; prioritize correctness and crawlable HTML.

ACCESSIBILITY
Semantic ruby and lang=ja spans; keyboard-operable drills, accessible audio/transcripts, visible focus,
reduced motion, high-contrast theme variants, responsive Japanese text with proper line breaking, and
answer review that doesn't rely only on colors or sound. Offer writing alternatives for motor limitations
and audio transcript alternatives for deaf learners. Speaking never blocks level completion.

VERSIONING / CHANGE SAFETY
Publish immutable IDs, explicit item revisions and migration policy for answer keys. If a published
question was wrong, annotate corrections, make invalid attempts regradable or voidable, and preserve
learner history with a clear explanation. Maintain editorial changelog, license/attribution register and
preview URLs for each content batch before publication.

==========================================================================================================
07 | COMPLETE MODULE, CHAPTER AND LESSON-OBJECTIVE MAP
==========================================================================================================
Read this section as BUILD TICKETS for curriculum writing; these are not completed lessons.
Each numbered chapter receives 8 explicitly described micro-lessons + 1 chapter checkpoint.
Vocab/grammar/kanji item allocation inside each chapter will be finalized in the vetted content inventory.

##########################################################################################################
L0  |  FOUNDATIONS / LEVEL 0  |  5 MODULES / 20 CHAPTERS
##########################################################################################################
Planning quota: ~130 new lexical entries; ~12 language targets; ~0 new kanji for reading recognition.
Each chapter: 8 micro-lessons, 1 checkpoint, 1 original core reading, 2 core audio assets, 1 writing and 1 optional speaking task.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
MODULE l0-m01 | Getting Oriented
Module purpose: Learn how Japanese sounds, writing systems and study tools work.
Module checkpoint: a cumulative unseen review of the four chapter Can-dos, with original reading/listening, a supported writing task and optional oral rehearsal.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

L0-M01-C01 | Japanese language map
CAN-DO: Explain how hiragana, katakana, kanji and romaji differ
LANGUAGE FOCUS: script roles; SOV pattern preview
VOCAB / SCRIPT DOMAIN: writing-system examples; classroom terms
INPUT GENRES: Reading: script-identification chart. Listening: narrated overview.
  L01  Context and meaning: Identify the setting, participant roles and purpose connected with this
    Can-do: Explain how hiragana, katakana, kanji and romaji differ.
  L02  Vocabulary and word study: Recognize, pronounce and retrieve key expressions from writing-system
    examples; classroom terms; distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter
    scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply script roles; SOV pattern preview to achieve
    the chapter Can-do; explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Practise recognition, typing and optional tracing of the kana and written forms
    introduced here; connect character forms to context from writing-system examples; classroom terms.
  L05  Reading practice: Read an original script-identification chart and Identify specified symbols and
    explicit details. Cite the phrase that supports each answer.
  L06  Listening practice: Listen to an original narrated overview; Identify phonemes, syllables or
    immediately stated information. Repeat without transcript, then verify with transcript.
  L07  Writing / typing: Create recognizable kana, a phrase, or a very short supported sentence
    demonstrating this Can-do: Explain how hiragana, katakana, kanji and romaji differ. Compare with a
    model and a criteria-based rubric.
  L08  Optional speaking / shadowing: Pronounce the featured sounds or rehearse a short survival
    expression in a situation where you must: Explain how hiragana, katakana, kanji and romaji differ.
    Rehearse from model audio; record only at learner request.
  CHK  Chapter checkpoint: Complete original mixed questions on script roles; SOV pattern preview,
    writing-system examples; classroom terms, the script-identification chart and narrated overview;
    review errors and add weak items to spaced repetition.

L0-M01-C02 | Japanese sound system
CAN-DO: Distinguish Japanese mora timing and five vowel qualities
LANGUAGE FOCUS: vowel length; mora timing; basic articulation
VOCAB / SCRIPT DOMAIN: vowels and sample syllables
INPUT GENRES: Reading: pronunciation map. Listening: minimal-pair audio.
  L01  Context and meaning: Identify the setting, participant roles and purpose connected with this
    Can-do: Distinguish Japanese mora timing and five vowel qualities.
  L02  Vocabulary and word study: Recognize, pronounce and retrieve key expressions from vowels and
    sample syllables; distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply vowel length; mora timing; basic articulation
    to achieve the chapter Can-do; explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Practise recognition, typing and optional tracing of the kana and written forms
    introduced here; connect character forms to context from vowels and sample syllables.
  L05  Reading practice: Read an original pronunciation map and Identify specified symbols and explicit
    details. Cite the phrase that supports each answer.
  L06  Listening practice: Listen to an original minimal-pair audio; Identify phonemes, syllables or
    immediately stated information. Repeat without transcript, then verify with transcript.
  L07  Writing / typing: Create recognizable kana, a phrase, or a very short supported sentence
    demonstrating this Can-do: Distinguish Japanese mora timing and five vowel qualities. Compare with a
    model and a criteria-based rubric.
  L08  Optional speaking / shadowing: Pronounce the featured sounds or rehearse a short survival
    expression in a situation where you must: Distinguish Japanese mora timing and five vowel qualities.
    Rehearse from model audio; record only at learner request.
  CHK  Chapter checkpoint: Complete original mixed questions on vowel length; mora timing; basic
    articulation, vowels and sample syllables, the pronunciation map and minimal-pair audio; review
    errors and add weak items to spaced repetition.

L0-M01-C03 | Sound changes and rhythm
CAN-DO: Recognize long vowels, small tsu and nasal mora
LANGUAGE FOCUS: long vowels; gemination; ん before different sounds
VOCAB / SCRIPT DOMAIN: common contrast pairs
INPUT GENRES: Reading: sound-contrast captions. Listening: audio discrimination.
  L01  Context and meaning: Identify the setting, participant roles and purpose connected with this
    Can-do: Recognize long vowels, small tsu and nasal mora.
  L02  Vocabulary and word study: Recognize, pronounce and retrieve key expressions from common contrast
    pairs; distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply long vowels; gemination; ん before different
    sounds to achieve the chapter Can-do; explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Practise recognition, typing and optional tracing of the kana and written forms
    introduced here; connect character forms to context from common contrast pairs.
  L05  Reading practice: Read an original sound-contrast captions and Identify specified symbols and
    explicit details. Cite the phrase that supports each answer.
  L06  Listening practice: Listen to an original audio discrimination; Identify phonemes, syllables or
    immediately stated information. Repeat without transcript, then verify with transcript.
  L07  Writing / typing: Create recognizable kana, a phrase, or a very short supported sentence
    demonstrating this Can-do: Recognize long vowels, small tsu and nasal mora. Compare with a model and
    a criteria-based rubric.
  L08  Optional speaking / shadowing: Pronounce the featured sounds or rehearse a short survival
    expression in a situation where you must: Recognize long vowels, small tsu and nasal mora. Rehearse
    from model audio; record only at learner request.
  CHK  Chapter checkpoint: Complete original mixed questions on long vowels; gemination; ん before
    different sounds, common contrast pairs, the sound-contrast captions and audio discrimination; review
    errors and add weak items to spaced repetition.

L0-M01-C04 | Study tools and input methods
CAN-DO: Enter Japanese using a keyboard and control reading aids
LANGUAGE FOCUS: IME input; furigana; polite classroom requests
VOCAB / SCRIPT DOMAIN: typing and study vocabulary
INPUT GENRES: Reading: keyboard guide. Listening: spoken instructions.
  L01  Context and meaning: Identify the setting, participant roles and purpose connected with this
    Can-do: Enter Japanese using a keyboard and control reading aids.
  L02  Vocabulary and word study: Recognize, pronounce and retrieve key expressions from typing and study
    vocabulary; distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply IME input; furigana; polite classroom requests
    to achieve the chapter Can-do; explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Practise recognition, typing and optional tracing of the kana and written forms
    introduced here; connect character forms to context from typing and study vocabulary.
  L05  Reading practice: Read an original keyboard guide and Identify specified symbols and explicit
    details. Cite the phrase that supports each answer.
  L06  Listening practice: Listen to an original spoken instructions; Identify phonemes, syllables or
    immediately stated information. Repeat without transcript, then verify with transcript.

L0-M05-C03 | First sentence patterns

VOCAB / SCRIPT DOMAIN: people, objects and everyday nouns

    Can-do: Recognize simple topic-comment statements and questions.
  L02  Vocabulary and word study: Recognize, pronounce and retrieve key expressions from people, objects
    and everyday nouns; distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply AはBです; か; は and を spelling preview to achieve
    the chapter Can-do; explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Practise recognition, typing and optional tracing of the kana and written forms
    introduced here; connect character forms to context from people, objects and everyday nouns.
  L05  Reading practice: Read an original mini self-intro and Identify specified symbols and explicit
    details. Cite the phrase that supports each answer.
  L06  Listening practice: Listen to an original basic Q&A; Identify phonemes, syllables or immediately
    stated information. Repeat without transcript, then verify with transcript.
  L07  Writing / typing: Create recognizable kana, a phrase, or a very short supported sentence
    demonstrating this Can-do: Recognize simple topic-comment statements and questions. Compare with a
    model and a criteria-based rubric.
  L08  Optional speaking / shadowing: Pronounce the featured sounds or rehearse a short survival
    expression in a situation where you must: Recognize simple topic-comment statements and questions.
    Rehearse from model audio; record only at learner request.
  CHK  Chapter checkpoint: Complete original mixed questions on AはBです; か; は and を spelling preview,

N5-M01-C01 | Hello and introductions
CAN-DO: Introduce yourself and identify another person's basic details
LANGUAGE FOCUS: です/じゃありません; は; か
VOCAB / SCRIPT DOMAIN: names, countries, occupations

  L01  Context and meaning: Identify the setting, participant roles and purpose connected with this
    Can-do: Introduce yourself and identify another person's basic details.
  L02  Vocabulary and word study: Recognize, pronounce and retrieve key expressions from names,
    countries, occupations; distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply です/じゃありません; は; か to achieve the chapter Can-do;
    explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Recognize, read and use the newly assigned everyday kanji through actual words,
    with kana support; connect character forms to context from names, countries, occupations.
  L05  Reading practice: Read an original profile cards and Find stated facts and simple referents. Cite
    the phrase that supports each answer.
  L06  Listening practice: Listen to an original first-meeting dialogues; Pick out the main topic and
    clearly stated facts from slow, clear speech. Repeat without transcript, then verify with transcript.
  L07  Writing / typing: Create 2-5 short connected Japanese sentences demonstrating this Can-do:
    Introduce yourself and identify another person's basic details. Compare with a model and a
    criteria-based rubric.
  L08  Optional speaking / shadowing: Use short memorized sentences in a simple interaction in a
    situation where you must: Introduce yourself and identify another person's basic details. Rehearse
    from model audio; record only at learner request.
  CHK  Chapter checkpoint: Complete original mixed questions on です/じゃありません; は; か, names, countries,

    Can-do: Choose simple polite or casual forms by situation.

    colleagues; distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply plain/polite switches; んだ to achieve the
    chapter Can-do; explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Read newly assigned kanji in chapter compounds, distinguish similar forms, and
    optionally handwrite target words; connect character forms to context from friends, colleagues.

    and chronology. Cite the phrase that supports each answer.

N3  |  INTERMEDIATE / N3-ALIGNED  |  12 MODULES / 48 CHAPTERS
##########################################################################################################
Planning quota: ~1650 new lexical entries; ~170 language targets; ~360 new kanji for reading recognition.

MODULE n3-m01 | More Natural Conversation
Module purpose: Develop connected, context-sensitive expression.
Module checkpoint: a cumulative unseen review of the four chapter Can-dos, with original reading/listening, a supported writing task and optional oral rehearsal.

N3-M01-C01 | Everyday nuance and softening
CAN-DO: Explain your view without sounding overly direct
LANGUAGE FOCUS: 〜みたい; 〜ようだ; 〜らしい intro
VOCAB / SCRIPT DOMAIN: opinions, impressions
INPUT GENRES: Reading: online comment. Listening: informal discussion.
  L01  Context and meaning: Identify the setting, participant roles and purpose connected with this
    Can-do: Explain your view without sounding overly direct.
  L02  Vocabulary and word study: Recognize, pronounce and retrieve key expressions from opinions,
    impressions; distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply 〜みたい; 〜ようだ; 〜らしい intro to achieve the chapter
    Can-do; explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Study kanji compounds, productive word formation, multiple useful readings and
    orthographic confusions; connect character forms to context from opinions, impressions.
  L05  Reading practice: Read an original online comment and Identify an author's claim, supporting
    details and straightforward implications. Cite the phrase that supports each answer.
  L06  Listening practice: Listen to an original informal discussion; Distinguish purpose, speaker stance
    and key evidence in connected speech. Repeat without transcript, then verify with transcript.
  L07  Writing / typing: Create a coherent 120-240-character Japanese paragraph demonstrating this
    Can-do: Explain your view without sounding overly direct. Compare with a model and a criteria-based
    rubric.
  L08  Optional speaking / shadowing: Sustain a brief explanation or question-and-response exchange in a
    situation where you must: Explain your view without sounding overly direct. Rehearse from model
    audio; record only at learner request.
  CHK  Chapter checkpoint: Complete original mixed questions on 〜みたい; 〜ようだ; 〜らしい intro, opinions,
    impressions, the online comment and informal discussion; review errors and add weak items to spaced
    repetition.

    Can-do: Track who believes what in a group exchange.
  L02  Vocabulary and word study: Recognize, pronounce and retrieve key expressions from speaker stance;
    distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply quoting; standpoint markers to achieve the
    chapter Can-do; explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Study kanji compounds, productive word formation, multiple useful readings and
    orthographic confusions; connect character forms to context from speaker stance.
  L05  Reading practice: Read an original group agenda and Identify an author's claim, supporting details
    and straightforward implications. Cite the phrase that supports each answer.
  L06  Listening practice: Listen to an original group discussion; Distinguish purpose, speaker stance
    and key evidence in connected speech. Repeat without transcript, then verify with transcript.
  L07  Writing / typing: Create a coherent 120-240-character Japanese paragraph demonstrating this
    Can-do: Track who believes what in a group exchange. Compare with a model and a criteria-based
    rubric.
  L08  Optional speaking / shadowing: Sustain a brief explanation or question-and-response exchange in a
    situation where you must: Track who believes what in a group exchange. Rehearse from model audio;
    record only at learner request.
  CHK  Chapter checkpoint: Complete original mixed questions on quoting; standpoint markers, speaker
    stance, the group agenda and group discussion; review errors and add weak items to spaced repetition.

N2-M01-C01 | Long noun phrases
CAN-DO: Parse heavily modified noun phrases efficiently
LANGUAGE FOCUS: embedded clauses; apposition; nominalization
VOCAB / SCRIPT DOMAIN: abstract noun phrases
INPUT GENRES: Reading: feature article. Listening: lecture.
  L01  Context and meaning: Identify the setting, participant roles and purpose connected with this
    Can-do: Parse heavily modified noun phrases efficiently.
  L02  Vocabulary and word study: Recognize, pronounce and retrieve key expressions from abstract noun
    phrases; distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply embedded clauses; apposition; nominalization to
    achieve the chapter Can-do; explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Infer meaning from kanji components and collocations; track register and
    near-synonymous compounds; connect character forms to context from abstract noun phrases.
  L05  Reading practice: Read an original feature article and Interpret tone, qualification, argument
    structure and cross-text relationships. Cite the phrase that supports each answer.
  L06  Listening practice: Listen to an original lecture; Follow coherent natural-speed explanations and
    contrasting perspectives. Repeat without transcript, then verify with transcript.
  L07  Writing / typing: Create a structured 250-450-character Japanese response demonstrating this
    Can-do: Parse heavily modified noun phrases efficiently. Compare with a model and a criteria-based
    rubric.
  L08  Optional speaking / shadowing: Explain a reasoned position, supported by examples, with
    appropriate register in a situation where you must: Parse heavily modified noun phrases efficiently.
    Rehearse from model audio; record only at learner request.
  CHK  Chapter checkpoint: Complete original mixed questions on embedded clauses; apposition;
    nominalization, abstract noun phrases, the feature article and lecture; review errors and add weak
    items to spaced repetition.

    and contrasting perspectives. Repeat without transcript, then verify with transcript.
  L07  Writing / typing: Create a structured 250-450-character Japanese response demonstrating this
    Can-do: Reconcile information across two sources. Compare with a model and a criteria-based rubric.

N1-M01-C01 | Thesis and hidden assumptions
CAN-DO: Identify a thesis and reconstruct implicit assumptions
LANGUAGE FOCUS: abstract nominalization; presupposition
VOCAB / SCRIPT DOMAIN: logic and argument nouns
INPUT GENRES: Reading: editorial. Listening: lecture argument.
  L01  Context and meaning: Identify the setting, participant roles and purpose connected with this
    Can-do: Identify a thesis and reconstruct implicit assumptions.
  L02  Vocabulary and word study: Recognize, pronounce and retrieve key expressions from logic and
    argument nouns; distinguish literal meaning from their actual usage in the chapter scenario.
  L03  Grammar and sentence function: Interpret and apply abstract nominalization; presupposition to
    achieve the chapter Can-do; explain one common misuse or ambiguity.
  L04  Script and kanji: Contrast rare and abstract kanji compounds, subtle orthographic choices and
    stylistic connotations; connect character forms to context from logic and argument nouns.
  L05  Reading practice: Read an original editorial and Evaluate implicit premises, detailed logic,
    rhetorical nuance and author stance. Cite the phrase that supports each answer.
  L06  Listening practice: Listen to an original lecture argument; Track argument, implicit intent and
    changing positions in rapid or complex speech. Repeat without transcript, then verify with
    transcript.
  L07  Writing / typing: Create a carefully qualified 400-800-character Japanese synthesis demonstrating
    this Can-do: Identify a thesis and reconstruct implicit assumptions. Compare with a model and a
    criteria-based rubric.
  L08  Optional speaking / shadowing: Present and defend a nuanced perspective, with a self-review of
    register and clarity in a situation where you must: Identify a thesis and reconstruct implicit
    assumptions. Rehearse from model audio; record only at learner request.
  CHK  Chapter checkpoint: Complete original mixed questions on abstract nominalization; presupposition,
    logic and argument nouns, the editorial and lecture argument; review errors and add weak items to
    spaced repetition.

==========================================================================================================
08 | SAMPLE PRODUCTION TICKET: N5-M01-C01
==========================================================================================================
TICKET ID: n5-m01-c01 | Hello and introductions
CAN-DO: Introduce yourself in short polite Japanese and identify another person's basic details.
PREREQUISITES: l0-m05-c04 recommended; kana input and pronunciation aids accessible.
TARGET FOR THIS TICKET: 15-25 new lexical entries, 2-3 teachable form contrasts; no more than
3-5 new kanji in reading words. These allocations are examples pending editorial inventory.
INPUT GENRES: 2 learner-created profile cards; 1 readable mini-dialogue; 2 short original recorded
first-meeting conversations with transcripts and controlled-speed playback.

LESSON 1: Show a scene with two people meeting; learner selects who, where and what is being exchanged.
LESSON 2: Introduce greeting/identity words with kana, pronunciation and 3 original usage examples.
LESSON 3: Explain topic は, nominal copula です, negation じゃありません and interrogative か with
          accessible examples; contrast a statement, a question and a polite negation.
LESSON 4: Read useful proper names and simple kanji-in-context (only pre-approved taught items).
LESSON 5: Read 2 short self-introduction cards and answer 4-6 factual questions with evidence.
LESSON 6: Listen to 2 first-meeting clips and choose identities/occupations from 4-6 questions.
LESSON 7: Type 3-4 self-introduction sentences; check word order, particles and politeness against
          a model; offer prompts to learners who prefer not to disclose personal identity.
LESSON 8: Optionally rehearse and record a 15-30 second fictional introduction with model audio.
CHECKPOINT: 12-16 original questions, at least 3 involving listening and 3 involving reading.
ANSWER KEY: versioned correct answer, distractor rationale, and feedback for every scored item.
CONTENT QA: editor + Japanese-language reviewer, transcript/playback review, license check.
PASS CONDITION: chapter can be completed regardless of score; recommend review under 80%.
REFERENCE LINKS: grammar/topic-particle-wa, grammar/desu, words and review cards actually taught.

EXAMPLE ASSET IDs
n5-m01-c01-l01 through n5-m01-c01-l08
read-n5-m01-c01-a; audio-n5-m01-c01-a; audio-n5-m01-c01-b
quiz-n5-m01-c01-v1; rubric-n5-m01-c01-write; cards auto-linked by item IDs

==========================================================================================================
09 | CONTENT PRODUCTION PIPELINE AND RELEASE GATES
==========================================================================================================
WORK SEQUENCE (DEPENDENCY ORDER)
PHASE A - approve the blueprint: course outcomes, level scope, chapter ordering, cross-skill priorities.
PHASE B - create source-of-truth inventories: distinct vocab senses, kanji, grammar, audio and task IDs;
          mark first introduction, review appearances and prerequisite dependencies.
PHASE C - ship Level 0: kana trainer, pronunciation, IME support, assessments and accessibility.
PHASE D - pilot one complete N5 module (4 chapters) with real audio, reading, word and kanji pages,
          listening questions, open-text writing and optional speaking. Test real learners' comprehension.
PHASE E - deliver all N5 chapters with weekly/module integrated reviews and two original mock forms.
PHASE F - extend through N4, N3, N2, N1 in sequence while incrementally expanding shared centers.
PHASE G - update continuously with learner diagnostics, reviewer corrections and new original practice.

PER-ASSET EDITORIAL STATUS
planned -> drafted -> internally checked -> Japanese-reviewed -> audio/rights checked ->
accessibility verified -> assessment validated -> published -> monitored -> revised.
Do not auto-publish AI-generated sentences, translations, distractors or grammatical claims without a
qualified reviewer. QA sampling is insufficient for official-sounding exam claims or tricky grammatical
nuance; all scored items need answer-key validation. Human reviewers should inspect register, naturalness,
furigana and whether the proposed form is actually idiomatic in the context.

QUALITY CHECKLIST FOR EACH CHAPTER
[ ] Can-do is observable and matched to the content and assessment.
[ ] All new words, readings, structures and examples have stable IDs and source/rights metadata.
[ ] Japanese grammar is accurate; English nuance is understandable; no misleading one-to-one mappings.
[ ] Each sentence sounds natural; examples are not clichéd, insensitive or overly stereotyped.
[ ] Appropriate handling of gender, age, honorifics and social context; culture notes avoid generalization.
[ ] At least one meaningful reading plus 2 independent listening sources, with fully checked transcripts.
[ ] Writing rubric and optional speaking model are realistic for the level.
[ ] Quiz distractors are plausible, unambiguous and explained; no duplicate right answers by mistake.
[ ] Feedback is constructive and links to referenced grammar/word/kanji pages.
[ ] Kana, furigana, punctuation and typographic line breaks render correctly on mobile/dark mode.
[ ] Audio has licensed usage rights, captions/transcripts, loading/error state and accessible controls.
[ ] No third-party copyrighted text/audio/question reused without permission and attribution.
[ ] All linked content exists, is approved for publication and remains navigable without a paid account.
[ ] Page has unique metadata, correct canonical URL, appropriate index/noindex and related-item links.
[ ] Automated checks pass; QA on mobile at 320, 375, 430, 768, 1024 and 1440 CSS pixels where possible.

ASSESSMENT CHECKLIST
[ ] Every question maps to a chapter/level objective and one or more knowledge item IDs.
[ ] Distractor difficulty is developmentally appropriate and not misleading.
[ ] Listening questions can actually be answered from the audio without unintended cues.
[ ] No answer is revealed by furigana, transcript default state, DOM metadata or screen-reader labels.
[ ] Answers and reasoning reviewed independently; question versions are tracked.
[ ] User receives useful feedback and can resume or retake without corrupting progress.
[ ] Practice scores are not represented as official scaled scores.

RELEASE ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
Before a level is shown as 'Complete', every required chapter must have approved assets, at least one
quality-checked reading and two listening tracks, accessible transcripts, working answers and feedback,
a cumulative module checkpoint, a capstone and the specified number of original mock forms. There must
be no empty placeholder chapter indexed as a finished lesson. Progress data migration and export/import
must work for newly introduced lesson/assessment types.

GOVERNANCE
Curriculum editor owns scope and sequence; Japanese-language reviewer owns accuracy; pronunciation/audio
reviewer owns recordings; assessment editor owns item validity; frontend engineer owns accessible lesson
rendering and storage; SEO editor owns page indexation, citations and link integrity. Keep signed-off
review status and content revisions in Git, with staged previews on Cloudflare Pages.

==========================================================================================================
10 | AUTHORITATIVE REFERENCES, ASSUMPTIONS AND OPEN EDITORIAL DECISIONS

OFFICIAL REFERENCES CHECKED ON 2026-11-01

[2] JLPT, Composition of Test Sections and Items:
    https://www.jlpt.jp/e/guideline/testsections.html
[3] JLPT, FAQ: no current published Test Content Specifications / exhaustive word-kanji-grammar list;
    no directly tested conversation or composition section:
    https://www.jlpt.jp/e/faq/
[4] JLPT, Can-do Self-Evaluation (learner survey reference, not syllabus):
    https://www.jlpt.jp/e/about/candolist.html
[5] JLPT, Scoring Sections and Results (official scaled scores; do not mimic with raw quiz percentages):
    https://www.jlpt.jp/e/guideline/results.html
[6] Japan Foundation, JF Standard for Japanese-Language Education: Can-do and task-focused design:
    https://www.jfstandard.jpf.go.jp/summaryen/ja/render.do
[7] Japan Foundation, Irodori overview (practical contextual course progression):
    https://yg.jpf.go.jp/what-is-irodori/

USE OF SOURCES
These references inform overall scope and assessment principles; the chapter names, sequencing,
asset counts, explicit lesson objectives, writing/speaking goals and internal readiness thresholds in
this blueprint are ORIGINAL FreeJapaneseAcademy planning proposals, not copied official standards.
The academy must produce original examples and tests or document licenses for any third-party resources.

EDITORIAL DECISIONS REQUIRING HUMAN REVIEW BEFORE MASS AUTHORING
A. Approve/correct 248 chapters and the sequencing of level-specific forms. Some forms are deliberately
   previewed before fully mastered; first exposure is not equivalent to claimed mastery.
B. Validate unique lexical and kanji inventories, avoiding false 'official JLPT vocabulary' labels.
C. Decide default lesson durations and realistic work quotas from pilot learner feedback.
D. Define allowable word/kanji drift between levels and rules for supplemental advanced vocab decks.
E. Decide how professionally produced or reviewed audio will be sourced at scale within the free model.
F. Establish native-proficient reviewer process, including compensation/volunteer governance and QA.
G. Confirm accessibility and privacy of writing/microphone features for minors and offline use.
H. Calibrate exam length, timing, item distribution and readiness rules separately per level using
   current official JLPT item specifications, not guesses in this document.
I. Plan natural Japanese examples that address the wide diversity of English-speaking learners.
J. Pilot Chapter N5-M01-C01 before approving a template for thousands of future lessons.

IMMEDIATE NEXT ACTION
Approve this Master Curriculum Blueprint as a planning baseline; create structured content-inventory
files and audit the first Level 0 module plus N5-M01 before full-scale writing. The present document
is intentionally an implementation-ready MAP, not a substitute for individual lesson texts or expert QA.
